The anchor $X=Y/W((8/e)Y)$ and the corrections are instances of theorems \repopath{p0:thm:lambert-core} and \repopath{p0:thm:perturbed-inversion} of the repository's transseries volume, and the threshold remark is the separation step of \repopath{p0:thm:staircase} (Part~0). No novelty is claimed for the inversion mechanics.
\end{writenote}

Write $\ell_k$ for the logarithmic correction coefficients in
Theorem~\ref{air:dfa:thm:main}. For each finite $M$ define a positive-real model
by exponentiating
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:inverse-model}
\mathcal F_M(x)=\log\gamma+\log\Gamma(x+1)+x\log8+3zx^{1/3}
+\tfrac78\log x+\sum_{k=1}^{M}\ell_k x^{-k/3}.
\end{equation}
Its derivative is $\log(8x)+O(x^{-2/3})$, positive for sufficiently large
$x$. Therefore $\mathcal F_M$ has a unique eventual inverse $x_M(Y)$.
No values of $b_n$ at nonintegers are asserted.

Put
\[
Y=\log y,\qquad w=W((8/\ee)Y),\qquad X=Y/w.
\]
The principal real Lambert function is used. Then $X$ solves
$X(\log X+\log8-1)=Y$. Stirling expansion gives
\[
\mathcal F_M(x)=x(\log x+\log8-1)+3zx^{1/3}
+\tfrac{11}{8}\log x+\log(\gamma\sqrt{2\pi})+O(x^{-1/3}).
\]
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:first-inverse}
x_M(Y)=X-
\frac{3zX^{1/3}+\frac{11}{8}\log X+\log(\gamma\sqrt{2\pi})}{1+w}
+O\!\left(\frac{X^{-1/3}}{\log X}\right).
\end{equation}
Indeed the correction is $O(X^{1/3}/\log X)$; the change in the stretched
term's derivative contributes $O(X^{-1/3}/(\log X)^2)$ to the index, and
the quadratic contribution from the factorial core is
$O(X^{-1/3}/(\log X)^3)$. The first omitted forward coefficient is at
most the remainder displayed in \eqref{air:dfa:eq:first-inverse}.

Higher corrections can be generated by reversion of
\eqref{air:dfa:eq:inverse-model}, or by Newton iteration
\[
x^{(r+1)}=x^{(r)}-
\frac{\mathcal F_M(x^{(r)})-Y}{\mathcal F_M'(x^{(r)})}.
\]
On $x\asymp X$, $\mathcal F_M''(x)=O(1/x)$ and
$\mathcal F_M'(x)\asymp\log X$, so each sufficiently accurate Newton
step squares the index error up to the factor $O((X\log X)^{-1})$.
Finite forward orders and finite Newton depth therefore give any desired
finite algebraic inverse accuracy. They remain distinct errors: solving
a fixed model more accurately does not improve its agreement with $b_n$.
Precisely, Theorem~\ref{air:dfa:thm:main} and the mean-value theorem imply
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:index-error}
x_M(\log b_n)-n=
O_M\!\left(\frac{n^{-(M+1)/3}}{\log n}\right).
\end{equation}
For arbitrary $y$, the first integer index with $b_n\ge y$ is a different
object. The asymptotics show eventual strict increase, since
$b_{n+1}/b_n\sim8n$, but a threshold still requires checking the adjacent
integers or a certified bracket. An unspecified big-$O$ constant is not a
certified finite threshold algorithm.

\section{Verification and numerical limitations}
The accompanying code separates analytic proof from finite evidence.
The exact script checks 5,050 triangular cells through $n=100$ against
the positive renewal construction, verifies 5,049 normalized recurrence
instances, includes the nonzero $m=1$ delay, and reproduces all 20 displayed
OEIS terms. The symbolic coefficient generator implements the triangular
polynomial inversion. A separate direct-linear-system computation checks
its coefficients without importing the generator. The analytic estimates
above, rather than these finite checks, establish the theorem.

For orientation only, direct floating evaluation of the normalized
amplitude gives approximately $178.317$, $164.945$, $150.038$, $136.080$,
and $131.985$ at $n=10,20,40,80,100$, respectively. These slowly varying
values are \emph{not} enclosures of $\gamma$, do not give certified digits,
and are not used in the proof. Inverse numerical tests, when a trial
amplitude is chosen, verify only the specified model with that trial
parameter.

The package contains this standalone article, its source and build script,
exact and symbolic verification programs and JSON outputs, a separate
mathematical review, bounded literature and overlap receipts, a clean
replay script, and a file manifest. The PDF build and all pages were
visually checked separately from mathematical review.
\begin{writenote}
This paragraph describes source 48's delivered archive (commit \texttt{096ee7b87}); its manuscript, PDF, build script and checksum manifest are not shipped. The README of this report lists the shipped files.
\end{writenote}


\section{Literature boundary and further questions}\label{air:dfa:sec:questions}
The earlier binary-DFA result \cite{dfa} provides the two-sided order,
not the positive limiting amplitude. The later general-arity paper
\cite{gdw} gives a broader exact recurrence and a relaxed-tree asymptotic
theorem. Its general-arity setting does not supply the present endpoint
amplitude. Ordinary minimal automata for arbitrary languages have different
asymptotics and should not be confused with this finite-language class.
The official entry \cite{oeis}, the cited papers, the author's current
project bibliography, and focused 2025--2026 searches were checked on
2 October 2026. No later amplitude theorem was located; this bounded
search is not a guarantee of universal novelty.
\begin{writenote}
The repository searches recorded in \repopath{48-dfa-literature-status.md} (at commit \texttt{a68b926cf}) were right when made. The same batch delivered the relaxed, compacted and fixed-arity companions, printed as Parts~\ref{air:part:rb}--\ref{air:part:ka} of this report; source 48 cites neither its binary companions nor their shared text.
\end{writenote}


Several extensions deserve separate investigation.
\begin{enumerate}
\item Obtain certified decimal enclosures of $\gamma$ by effective error
bounds or positive interval propagation. Existence alone does not do this.
\item For a normalized exclusion coefficient $(\alpha m+\beta)$, the same
formal drift is $\alpha/(4N)$, suggesting power $1-\alpha/4$.
The relaxed, compacted, and present DFA cases have parameters
$(0,0)$, $(1,-1)$, and $(1/2,0)$, respectively. Nonnegative renewal
weights and upper domination can hold on a larger parameter set, but a
matching lower bound or another positivity proof is still required.
No general parameter theorem is claimed here.
\item Extend the method to the non-nearest-neighbor general-arity
recurrences, controlling the non-selfadjoint modes rather than assuming
that the binary Jacobi argument automatically applies.
\begin{writenote}
For every fixed alphabet size $k\ge3$ this is done in Part~\ref{air:part:ka} (source 18), with a fixed weighted norm and a summable delay instead of the binary Jacobi argument, as this item anticipates.
\end{writenote}

\item Analyze exponentially small corrections beyond the present
finite-order Poincar\'e expansion.
\end{enumerate}

\appendix
\part*{Appendix}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Appendix}

\section{Self-contained Jacobi estimates}\label{air:app:jacobi}
\begin{writenote}
This appendix is source 15's (Part~\ref{air:part:cb}); source 48 ships the same text with ``DFA theorem'' in its first sentence. ``Section~3'' of source 15 is Section~\ref{air:cb:sec:jacobi} here. The appendix serves Parts~\ref{air:part:cb} and~\ref{air:part:dfa}, and is a second, self-contained proof of the estimates of Part~\ref{air:part:rb}, Sections~\ref{air:rb:exact-recurrence-and-contractive-symmetrization}--\ref{air:rb:convergence-of-the-scalar-amplitude}.
\end{writenote}


This appendix proves all shared gauge, gap, profile, projection and spectral
product estimates used in the compacted theorem. Its objects are the explicit
matrices in Section~\ref{air:cb:sec:jacobi}. The relaxed all-orders theorem is not assumed here.

\subsection{Exact symmetrization and norm contraction}
For the relaxed recurrence define
\[
d_{N,j}=\frac{r_{(N+j)/2,(N-j)/2}}{((N+j)/2)!}
\]
on the physical support and zero otherwise. Direct substitution gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:relaxed-step}
d_{0,j}=\mathbf1_{j=0},\quad d_{N,-1}=0,\quad
d_{N,j}=\frac{N-j+2}{N+j}d_{N-1,j-1}+d_{N-1,j+1}.
\end{equation}
For $v_N^{(r)}(j)=d_{N,j}/D_N(j)$ the factorial identities
\[
\frac{D_N(j)}{D_N(j-1)}=b_{N,j},\qquad
\frac{D_{N-1}(j)}{D_N(j)}=Q_N(j)
\]
on their required supports prove $v_N^{(r)}=S_NQ_Nv_{N-1}^{(r)}$.
All edge and diagonal coefficients lie in $[0,1]$.
The finite Jacobi matrix is irreducible, so its largest eigenvalue has a
positive unit eigenvector. The matrix exchanges the two parity subspaces,
so its spectrum is symmetric about zero and $\norm{S_N}=\lambda_N$.
Consequently $\norm{S_NQ_N}\le\lambda_N$ and
$\norm{v_N^{(r)}/P_N}\le1$.

\subsection{The coarse Airy limit and its gap}
Write $\eps=N^{-1/3}$ and $x_j=(j+1)\eps$. For vectors supported on
$0,\ldots,N+1$, the exact quadratic form is
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:form}
\begin{aligned}
\ip{v}{(2I-S_N)v}={}&\sum_{j=0}^{N}b_{N,j+1}|v_{j+1}-v_j|^2+|v_0|^2\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{N}(2-b_{N,j}-b_{N,j+1})|v_j|^2
+(2-b_{N,N+1})|v_{N+1}|^2.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The term $|v_0|^2$ is the first Dirichlet-gradient interval, with fictitious
value $v_{-1}=0$. No nonphysical edge coefficient $b_{N,0}$ is needed.
For $1\le j\le N+1$,
\[
1-b_{N,j}=\frac{2(j-1)}{(N+j)(1+b_{N,j})}\ge\frac{j-1}{N+j}.
\]
Thus for universal $c,C>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:coercivity}
\eps^{-2}\ip{v}{(2I-S_N)v}
\ge c\sum_{j=0}^{N+1}x_j|v_j|^2-C\eps\norm v^2.
\end{equation}
On every fixed $x$ window the edge weights tend uniformly to one and
\[
\eps^{-2}(2-b_{N,j}-b_{N,j+1})\longrightarrow2x_j
\]
uniformly there. Interpolate linearly with value $\eps^{-1/2}v_j$ at $x_j$
and zero at the origin; add a zero node after the last finite node and
extend by zero. The terminal term of \eqref{air:cb:eq:form} controls the final
interpolation interval. Bounded scaled energy gives local $H^1$ bounds.
Equation \eqref{air:cb:eq:coercivity} gives uniformly small $L^2$ tails outside
large fixed windows. Local compactness followed by this tail estimate gives
strong $L^2$ compactness; the boundary interval enforces zero trace at zero.
The limiting lower form is
\[
\int_0^\infty\bigl(|f'(x)|^2+2x|f(x)|^2\bigr)\,dx.
\]
Norm convergence follows on a fixed window from the gradient bound and
Riemann sums, then globally from \eqref{air:cb:eq:coercivity}. Sampling smooth,
compactly supported functions vanishing at zero supplies the reverse upper
form bound. Applying the min--max principle in both directions proves
convergence of every fixed low eigenvalue of $\eps^{-2}(2I-S_N)$ to the
corresponding eigenvalue of
\[
\mathcal H=-\frac{d^2}{dx^2}+2x,\qquad f(0)=0.
\]
These eigenvalues are $\mu_k=-2^{2/3}z_k$, where
$z_1=z>z_2>\cdots$ are the negative Airy zeros. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:airy-gap}
\lambda_N=2+2a\eps^2+o(\eps^2),\qquad
\lambda_N-\lambda_{2,N}\sim2^{2/3}(z-z_2)\eps^2,
\end{equation}
where $\lambda_{2,N}$ is the second-largest positive eigenvalue for large
$N$. Between opposite parity subspaces, the singular values of $S_N$ are
its positive eigenvalues, possibly with additional zeros. Removing the top
singular direction therefore gives norm at most
$\lambda_N(1-cN^{-2/3})$. The negative eigenvalue $-\lambda_N$ is not a
second slow direction within occupied parity.

\subsection{A finite quantitative quasimode}
The Airy equation is $F''=2(x+a)F$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:app-quasimode}
\begin{aligned}
F_2(x)&=\frac{2(-a+2x)}{15}F(x)+\frac{x(2a+x)}{15}F'(x),\\
F_3(x)&=\frac{F(x)-xF'(x)}3,\\
f_N(x)&=F(x)+\eps^2F_2(x)+\eps^3F_3(x),\\
\widehat\lambda_N&=2+2a\eps^2+3\eps^3
+\frac{13a^2}{15}\eps^4+\frac{5a}{3}\eps^5.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Choose a fixed smooth cutoff $\chi$, equal to one on $[0,1]$ and zero on
$[2,\infty)$, and let $\widehat f_N$ be the $\ell^2$ normalization of
$\chi(j/N^{1/2})f_N(x_j)$. Changing or omitting the cutoff in local
calculations gives an error smaller than every inverse power of $N$.

For a direct residual check, the two edge coefficients at a sample point are
\[
b_-(x,\eps)=\sqrt{\frac{1-x\eps^2+3\eps^3}{1+x\eps^2-\eps^3}},\qquad
b_+(x,\eps)=\sqrt{\frac{1-x\eps^2+2\eps^3}{1+x\eps^2}}.
\]
Expand
\[
b_-f_N(x-\eps)+b_+f_N(x+\eps)-\widehat\lambda_Nf_N(x)
\]
through degree five in $\eps$, reducing derivatives by $F''=2(x+a)F$.
Every coefficient vanishes. The omitted left sample is zero because
$F(0)=F_2(0)=F_3(0)=0$. On the cutoff support,
$x\eps^2=O(N^{-1/2})$, and Taylor remainders are bounded by
$C\eps^6$ times a fixed polynomial times Airy decay. Such envelopes are
square-integrable. Cutoff derivatives contribute only
$\exp(-cN^{1/4})$ times powers of $N$. The unnormalized sampled norm is
asymptotic to $\eps^{-1/2}\norm F_{L^2}$, so
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:app-residual}
\norm{(S_N-\widehat\lambda_N)\widehat f_N}=O(\eps^6).
\end{equation}
The coarse gap identifies the nearby eigenvalue as $\lambda_N$; projecting
onto the complementary eigenspaces gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:app-approx}
\lambda_N=\widehat\lambda_N+O(\eps^6),\qquad
\norm{\psi_N-\widehat f_N}=O(\eps^4).
\end{equation}
The sign follows from the positive leading Airy profile.

The normalized samples have $\ell^2$ derivative $O(N^{-1})$ with respect
to continuous $N$, and one-site shift difference $O(N^{-1/3})$.
They have bounded polynomial moments on the Airy scale. Together with
\eqref{air:cb:eq:app-approx}, these facts give
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:app-consequences}
\norm{\psi_N-\psi_{N-1}}=O(N^{-1}),\qquad
\norm{(I-Q_N)\psi_N}=O(N^{-4/3}),\qquad
\psi_N(0)=\sqrt2 N^{-1/2}(1+O(N^{-1/3})).
\end{equation}
For the diagonal estimate use $1-\sqrt{1-t}\le t$ on $[0,1]$, the sampled
moments, and \eqref{air:cb:eq:app-approx}. For the endpoint use
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:airy-integral}
I=\int_0^\infty F(x)^2\,dx=2^{-1/3}\Ai'(z)^2,\qquad
\frac{F'(0)}{\sqrt I}=\sqrt2.
\end{equation}
The integral identity follows by differentiating
$u\Ai(u)^2-\Ai'(u)^2$ and integrating from $z$ to infinity.
The derivative $\Ai'(z)$ is positive for the largest zero. A Riemann-sum
estimate with integrable derivative gives relative normalization error
$O(\eps)$. The endpoint Taylor error is $O(\eps^2)$, and the normalized
quasimode error divided by the leading endpoint is $O(\eps^{5/2})$.
This proves the endpoint assertion. The needed Airy decay and polynomially
weighted integrability are standard consequences of its decaying ODE
solution; see \cite{dlmf}.

\subsection{Projection and adjoint estimates}
Writing $S_N\psi_N=\lambda_N\psi_N$ on its two parity components and taking
the corresponding scalar products shows that both components have equal
squared norm. Thus $g_N,h_N$ in \eqref{air:cb:eq:ground-defs} are unit vectors and
$S_Nh_N=\lambda_Ng_N$, $S_Ng_N=\lambda_Nh_N$. The estimates above imply
\begin{equation}\label{air:cb:eq:app-parity}
\norm{h_N-g_{N-1}}=O(N^{-1}),\qquad
\norm{(I-Q_N)h_N}+\norm{(I-Q_N)g_{N-1}}=O(N^{-4/3}).
\end{equation}
For the second term use the explicit sampled approximation at $N-1$,
rather than the weaker $O(N^{-1})$ profile difference. Its Airy moments
with $Q_N$ give the same bound, and its norm error is already
$O(N^{-4/3})$.

On the preceding parity,
\[
\Pi_NS_N=S_N(I-|h_N\rangle\langle h_N|).
\]
The singular gap therefore gives, for every such $y$,
\[
\norm{\Pi_NM_Ny}\le(1-cN^{-2/3})\norm{Q_Ny}
\le(1-cN^{-2/3})\norm y.
\]
It is unnecessary to assume $y\perp h_N$. Furthermore
\[
\norm{M_Ng_{N-1}-g_N}=O(N^{-1}),\qquad
M_N^*g_N=Q_Nh_N,
\]
so \eqref{air:cb:eq:app-parity} also gives the adjoint-ground bound.
Finally,
\[
t_N=\ip{Q_Nh_N}{g_{N-1}}=1+O(N^{-4/3}).
\]
Indeed the diagonal defect is $O(N^{-4/3})$, and the unit-vector identity
$\langle h,g\rangle=1-\norm{h-g}^2/2$ makes the profile overlap defect
$O(N^{-2})$. These prove every projection estimate in
Lemma~\ref{air:cb:lem:jacobi}.

\subsection{The positive spectral product constant}
From \eqref{air:cb:eq:app-approx},
\[
\log\lambda_N=\log2+aN^{-2/3}+\tfrac32N^{-1}+O(N^{-4/3}).
\]
Integral or Euler summation for the first two powers and absolute convergence
of the residual series give
\[
P_N=\kappa2^N\exp(3aN^{1/3})N^{3/2}(1+O(N^{-1/3})),\qquad\kappa>0.
\]
For completeness, $\kappa$ is the positive limit of the quotient in this
formula. Each factor $\lambda_N$ is positive, and the logarithm of the
quotient converges to a finite real number. This completes the proof of
Lemma~\ref{air:cb:lem:jacobi}.
